# High-quality realtime performance handover

## Shared collider hierarchy preparation

[Invocation-local validated prefixes](collider-hierarchy-prefixes-2026-10-25.md)
share rootless collider ancestry, transform composition and dirty state while
retaining the full depth limit and native arithmetic order. Independent pairs
take 74–80% less time for ancestor/body changes and retain exact fingerprints.
All 17 owner checks and the production invalid-rotation terminal-fault gate
pass. Normal Summer A/B/A coupled medians are 5.033/4.738/5.652 ms with 72
matched source hashes. Griffin reaches sleeping four-pad handoff with an
identical final body query; Tesla's stationary readiness window stays clear.
Host variation and the existing Tesla datum HUD remain qualification limits.

The source-matched native capture verifies framing, exports, the owned
PID/connection and 1 kHz samples. Collider preparation is 0.257/0.506 ms
median/p95, versus 0.622/0.933 ms baseline; complete instrumented FixedMain is
6.049 ms. Native physics schedule and physical solar visibility remain measured
leads at 1.399 and 0.658 ms respectively. Exact-FixedMain CPU data is retained
for further attribution. The normal binary is restored. All original targets
remain open; no dependency changes or quality reductions are introduced.

## Physics pose selection

[The frame-local pose selection repair](physics-pose-invalidation-2026-10-25.md)
excludes plain leaves from ancestor invalidation while retaining their exact
representation history, and stops selection at the active frame. Independent
consumer pairs reduce render-only selection work by 15–97%, with actual
physical-parent updates unchanged. All 16 owner checks and the production
invalid-rotation terminal-fault gate pass. Normal Summer A/B/A coupled medians
are 5.233/4.948/5.353 ms with all 72 source hashes matched. Griffin reaches
sleeping four-pad handoff; Tesla's stationary readiness window stays clear.
Host contention, the existing Tesla datum HUD and the limited mission windows
are recorded in the review. Loading, coupled-tick and graphics targets remain
unmet. Rootless collider propagation is the next separately sampled lead.

The matching native capture verifies its owned PID/connection, 1 kHz sampling,
framing and exports. In 240 complete FixedMain ticks, pose-reader median falls
from 0.343 to 0.265 ms over 480 calls; parent-lookup samples fall from 61 to 25.
Complete instrumented FixedMain stays 6.940/6.920 ms, and rootless propagation
remains 0.622 ms. This is attribution evidence, not clean runtime acceptance.

## Native procedural solar enclosures

[The native enclosure repair](terrain-procedural-enclosures-2026-10-25.md)
tightens signed crater-band bounds without changing the sampled terrain or
adding a spatial index. Verified independent consumer pairs take 75% less time
at two-metre raster spacing; the one-metre case is 1–12% slower. The new
200-interval regression fails on the baseline and passes on the candidate;
eleven terrain mechanism checks, two core enclosure checks and all four
production solar gates pass. Summer's source-matched normal A/B/A coupled
medians are 5.604/4.888/5.752 ms. Griffin reaches sleeping four-pad handoff with
clear diagnostics. Host/workload-qualified graphics and loading targets remain
unmet.

The saved native capture matches the candidate source cohort and passes framing,
native exports and exact-zone PID/period checks. Solar visibility is
0.740/1.053 ms median/p95, while complete FixedMain remains 6.940 ms with Tracy.
Pose bridge and rootless collider propagation are the next first-party physics
leads; source-read sharing remains open after its earlier comparison was excluded.

## Physical solar artifact audit

[The exact-zone lookup audit](terrain-occlusion-lookup-2026-10-25.md) resolves
96 of 237 physical-solar CPU samples to native table lookup and key comparison;
the key's derive line alone did not establish hashing cost. The cache-layout
comparison is excluded: its test snapshots shared an inode and a later build
overwrote both variants. Neither cache-layout experiment remains. Ten numerical
mechanism checks passed for those experiments. The retained moving-ray
diagnostic now covers one- and two-metre cells. Any future larger-region index
would need proper asynchronous preparation, including the web worker boundary;
the retained repair stays at the procedural enclosure owner. All targets remain open.

## Native CPU instance preparation

[The source-matched native-builder comparison](native-instance-builder-2026-10-25.md)
selects Bevy's CPU preparation for the desktop composition. Normal frame medians
are 9.816 ms Griffin, 5.173 ms Summer and 5.781 ms Tesla, preserving authored
quality and passing point-light/Sun pixel gates. Tesla's GPU median remains
lower; both modes fit its measured frame budget. The two qualified Tracy captures
attribute Summer's GPU overhead to native bind-group and shadow-buffer preparation.
Normal loading and coupled-tick targets remain unmet, and Griffin's frame tail
still exceeds 10 ms. Physical solar visibility is the next sampled first-party
coupled-tick lead. All owned sessions close; raw data stays ignored.

## Native shared-shadow allocation

[The allocation repair](shared-shadow-allocation-2026-10-25.md) restores Bevy's
required native stage before bin unpacking for point/spot shadow roots. The
baseline passes authored setup but fails its GPU shadow probe; the corrected
normal build passes both positive probes without red pixels. Native ordering
and the owned production pixel gate pass. Griffin lands with clear diagnostics;
concurrent Tesla video rendering qualifies its slower frame timings. This is
a correctness prerequisite for further native-builder comparisons, not a timing
gain. All original targets remain open.

## Coupled certification readiness

The coordinator's existing ECS condition now waits on participant constructor
outcomes before running full certification. [The focused review](coupled-certification-readiness-2026-10-24.md)
records the low-level gate, 46 authored checks, the inconsistent-equations
negative fixture and an inspected normal Griffin touchdown/handoff. The
source-matched startup captures observe 274 -> 19 coordinator executions and
665.214 -> 56.500 ms of main-thread work. Certification samples and validates
the complete current graph at final admission.

Normal readiness is 17.517 s and does not establish an end-to-end speedup;
unchanged native construction also takes longer before the changed gate can
run. Complete coupled tick is 4.052/4.870 ms median/p95 and graphics
11.876/15.645 ms. Source hashes remain equal and registered diagnostics are
clear. Concurrent workloads, including video encoding in the diagnostic
capture, qualify timing comparisons. All original targets remain open.

## Indexed runtime output endpoints

`declare_map` now accepts the map's stored type and an owning backend's
entry predicate. Runtime output aliases use that same indexed selection and
live-slot ordering as scalar maps; their predicate requires the selected
endpoint's current `Port` component. The separate runtime-output enumeration
is removed. Scalar owners retain declarations before their first sample, and
direction rejection happens before entry validation. This is an extension of
the existing declaration owner, with no additional lookup table or cache.

All 25 port-core and eleven co-simulation port-owner tests pass. The existing
manual write benchmark remains ignored. The actual runtime-output backend's
diagnostic performs 512 reads on 8,192 endpoints in 219.318 ms before and
0.374 ms after; it also checks immediate endpoint removal. Scalar selected-read
cost remains 0.208 ms in the same owner run. The regular production build
passes in 2m 40s, and the production port-ownership scene passes all twelve
checks, including its negative cases.

The `*-selected-endpoint-map-unprofiled/` diagnostic sessions report:

| Twin | Admission (s) | Complete tick median/p95 (ms) | Frame median/p95 (ms) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Tesla | 5.524 | 3.003 / 9.283 | 10.091 / 40.698 |
| Summer | 3.407 | 3.987 / 4.477 | 5.460 / 10.777 |
| Griffin | 24.741 | 8.337 / 17.077 | 27.943 / 62.828 |

Authored hashes match the previous candidate within and between each run,
retained diagnostics and script-error checks are clear, and final images are
inspected. Each complete-tick result has 240 samples. Workloads differ: retained
snapshots include another worktree's Cargo/Rustc activity. Griffin still has one
pending visual tile at the final snapshot. These observations do not establish
a causal whole-scene gain, clean graphics acceptance or completed missions.
All owned sessions are closed, and all original targets remain open. Raw data
and the `selected-endpoint-map-gates/` receipts remain ignored in the same
evidence root as the preceding cohort.

The retained Summer Tracy capture identifies another steady cost:
`publish_sun_visibility` has 211 complete late-window zones with a median
1.428 ms, p95 2.020 ms and maximum 2.534 ms. Its exact CPU export matches those
211 zones and contains 310 samples, with 158 at array equality. The concrete
array/key type is not resolved by those line-table samples; cache comparison
cost is an attribution lead. No solar algorithm, precision, budgets or
certificates have changed. Raw zone/sample exports remain ignored
under `summer-structural-shape-profile/`.

## Selected port inspection

`lunco-port-core` carries exact-name selections into `PortDeclarationQuery`.
Declarations exclude unrequested identities before copying names or sampling
metadata and values. The map-backed owner uses its existing name index and
sorts selected entries by live slot order. Every direction and backend row for
a selected name remains visible; ordinary full inspection and named write
admission retain their contracts. No additional index or retained cache is added.

All 25 port-owner tests pass. The isolated diagnostic performs 512 reads,
selecting two live values and one absent name from an 8,192-port surface. It
measures 324.813 ms before and 0.214 ms after, preserving declaration order and
exact values. The regular production rebuild passes in 2m 52s, with the existing
USD unused-import warning. The production port-ownership gate passes all twelve
checks, including its negative cases. Its owned API session is closed.

The regular build without Tracy establishes this baseline and candidate:

| Twin | Admission before/after (s) | Complete tick median before/after (ms) | Candidate tick p95 (ms) | Frame median before/after (ms) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Tesla | 3.699 / 2.974 | 3.311 / 2.695 | 3.570 | 10.009 / 8.455 |
| Summer | 6.129 / 5.009 | 5.819 / 5.429 | 6.614 | 16.043 / 14.643 |
| Griffin | 25.074 / 21.166 | 8.704 / 8.401 | 9.944 | 24.597 / 22.855 |

Each complete-tick result is the owner's final 240-sample window. Authored
source hashes match within and between each pair, retained diagnostics and
script-error checks are clear, and final images are inspected. DEM stream and
derived work are settled at the final snapshots. These are short admission
observations, not completed mission or exact native-state replay. Another
LunCoSim graphics session remains active, and native cache contents are not
frozen; startup differences cannot be attributed solely to this reader change.
All six owned applications are closed. All strict loading, tick, graphics and
mission targets remain open. Continue profiling the consuming query/behavior
path and native construction rather than extrapolating the isolated benchmark.

Raw receipts remain ignored under
`.cache/codex-evidence/performance-20261023/`: `*-structural-shape-unprofiled/`,

## Exact proof shape keys

The published Rumoca `d47a5bf3` change moves shared-value operation identity
into `shared_values/shape.rs`. Complete typed IR encodings retain floating-point
bits; a hash lookup still compares the complete key. Slot reads have distinct
typed variants, and proof IDs follow program-order insertion within their
owning table. The output and final-slot checks retain their independent table.

The new regression demonstrates that the baseline checker accepted a change
to a constant's NaN payload bits. The current checker rejects it while retaining
destination-independent sharing. All 422 owner tests and the owner-only
all-feature Clippy check pass. The fixed twenty-model MSL canary preserves every
phase/error and channel-band row: twelve high-agreement traces, six known DAE
refusals and two known solve refusals, with no skipped or missing candidate
traces. This is Tier 1 qualification.
The worker's proof-failure counter is zero for all fourteen attempted
simulations in both cohorts; the encoding change introduces no observed
single-program proof refusals in this canary.

The scalar diagnostic measures 7.067 ms before and 6.621 ms after, while the
wide diagnostic measures 4.521 ms and 5.206 ms. The focused baseline and full
owner suite have different scopes and concurrent workloads, so these values do
not establish a performance gain. The application's nonlinear-array native/
reference test passes one hundred input changes, identical clock bits and
complete reported values within `1e-12`. All eleven manifest pins and twenty-nine
locked source identities use the published revision; the only additional graph
edge is the existing `bincode 1.3.3` dependency of `rumoca-ir-solve`. Unrelated
Windows lockfile edges are preserved. The regular Tracy build passes in
29m 52s. Its production drivetrain gate passes eight checks; planetary flight
and contact pass fourteen checks at tick 8103; scoped escape containment passes
five checks. The negative observation fixture reaches the expected terminal
`modelica-step-failed` fault without a process panic. All four owned test
sessions are closed; their receipts remain ignored in `structural-shape-gates/`.
The three `*-structural-shape-profile/` Tracy captures have terminal native
total/self/unwrapped/GPU exports, matching compressed framing, owned PIDs and
1 ms sampling periods. Their source hashes remain unchanged, retained diagnostics
are clear, and final images are inspected. Admission takes 3.908 s for Tesla,
5.880 s for Summer and 31.387 s for Griffin. Complete-tick medians are
4.900/7.266/11.087 ms. Griffin's coupled constructor still takes 8.384 s with
16,414 native hits out of 16,482 requests, so no construction gain is established.
Its exact constructor CPU window contains 4,541 stack samples and one matched
zone. Symbolic key/term work and operation cloning remain visible.
All owned applications, collectors and exporters are closed. Clean performance
and completed-mission qualification remain open.
The integrated base at `9d90d03d1`
includes the offline renderer, shared plot time axes and planetary-flight
contact support; preserve these changes and treat the next capture as a fresh
cohort. The planetary-flight review's authored-velocity frame-admission and
high-energy articulated-impact findings still require physics-owner
reproduction; its admitted-force gate does not certify those cases.

## Native compiler cache attribution

Opt-in trace events in `worker/native_code_cache.rs` expose the actual cache
path, limits and serialized get/insert order. They retain compiler keys and byte
counts, without artifact contents or simulation state. Normal info-level runs
do not emit them. The existing native-cache owner test passes; the regular
Tracy production rebuild passes in 2m 34s.

Exact LRU replay of `griffin-native-cache-key-profile` verifies every recorded
hit, eviction, retained count and byte total. All 32,768 disk entries load before
the first constructor lookup. The run requests 28,289 distinct keys occupying
38,244,875 bytes, within both existing limits. Its coupled initializer has
8,894 misses for keys never admitted in that process and 2,168 misses for
previously evicted keys. There are no concurrent duplicate misses. The saved
record contains every key requested by this run, so its publication completes.
These results do not justify increasing retention or adding single-flight
compilation for this workload.

A subsequent new-process run, `griffin-native-cache-key-warm-rebuilt-profile`,
reuses 36,904 of 37,040 lookups across all constructors. The coupled initializer
reuses 16,414 of 16,482 lookups, compiles 68 artifacts and rejects none. Its
session construction takes 8.307 s, versus 13.027 s in the preceding key trace;
end-to-end physics admission is 32.755 s versus 36.194 s. Sources remain stable
within and between these runs. Their requested key sets differ by 136 additions
and 140 removals. The shared cache file also changes between the first owned
exit and the second launch, so this is not a controlled cache-identical timing
comparison. Another LunCoSim session from the `tutorials` worktree uses the same
absolute default cache path; the writer of each intervening change is not
identified. Snapshot replacement can discard another process's additions, but
its contribution to the original miss surge is unproven.

Both captures have terminal native CSV exports, verified framing, owned PIDs
and 1 ms CPU sampling periods. Runtime diagnostics are clear and final images
show Griffin above lunar terrain; they do not establish landing acceptance.
Both owned applications and collectors are closed. Raw captures, operation
logs, cache fingerprints and replay summaries remain ignored. Native-cache
reuse is confirmed; sub-second loading and sub-millisecond coupled ticks remain
open. The remaining initializer CPU samples include symbolic proof construction
and Debug formatting even with almost all native artifacts reused.

## Unified live port declarations and slots

`lunco-port-core::PortDeclarationQuery` publishes a borrowed name, direction and
optional process-local slot together. The same owner callback supplies full
inspection and exact-name discovery; inspection retains that slot in its handle.
Live write admission queries each candidate once and consumes the winning
direction without another declaration lookup. Metadata, bounds, writability,
revision, slot identity and atomic commit retain their existing checks. Every
backend and caller uses this contract. It adds no cache, clock or Rhai policy;
this is generic engine dispatch and validation.

All 24 port-core tests pass. The diagnostic admission benchmark measures
131,072 writes through sixteen preceding owners at 20.996 ms before and
13.811 ms after. This is not a whole-tick result. The five affected caller test
packages compile, including the corrected Avian native-velocity fixtures with
explicit f32-to-f64 expectations. The regular Tracy production build passes in
3m 58s with the same pre-existing USD unused-import warning.

Local main's independent diagram navigation/presentation change (`faf8b1132`)
is fast-forwarded without overlapping these owners. The integrated regular
production rebuild passes in 5m 01s. An owned schema session verifies all 275
settled commands, including `ConfigureDiagramPresentation` and both optional
boolean fields; the generated command reference consumes that runtime schema.
The physics gates and captures in this section precede that UI integration;
their coupling code and inputs are unchanged.

The authored ownership gate passes all twelve checks, including refusal to
invent a `piloted` sample on a root without an input surface. The same negative
check fails on the previous binary. The drivetrain passes eight checks and the
non-finite observation reaches its expected terminal fault without a process
panic. Gate receipts remain ignored in `port-declaration-gates/`.

The `*-port-declaration-profile/` diagnostic runs have stable sources, clear
retained diagnostics, inspected final images, and verified native capture
framing, owned PIDs, 1 ms sampling periods and terminal CSV exports. Sources also
match their preceding captured Twin cohorts. Concurrent builds and LunCoSim
sessions remain active; cache contents and compilation counts differ.

| Twin | Admission (s) | Complete FixedMain median/p95 (ms) |
|---|---:|---:|
| Tesla | 3.242 | 4.009 / 5.278 |
| Summer | 7.944 | 9.296 / 21.575 |
| Griffin | 61.790 | 11.891 / 23.805 |

No controlled scene speedup follows from these captures. Griffin's write span
averages 2.072 ms; its coupled initializer takes 29.790 s with 5,499 hits and

## Shared unit-contract equality

`lunco-engineering-values::Unit` has private conversion coefficients validated
as finite by every constructor and deserializer. Its equality is therefore
reflexive. The `Eq` contract propagates through immutable `UnitReference`
definitions, allowing standard-library `Arc` equality to recognize shared
definitions by identity. Independently created definitions still compare their
complete values. Port writes retain all live owner, metadata and batch checks.

The regular targeted engineering-value run passes all twelve tests, including
finite extremes, signed zero, changed angular/relative semantics, independent
definitions and non-finite rejection. The ignored diagnostic benchmark measures
4,194,304 shared-reference comparisons at 25,128,264 ns before and 3,138,082 ns
after. Logs are ignored `shared-unit-equality-{baseline,qualified}.log` under
`.cache/codex-evidence/performance-20261023/`. This isolates one contract cost;
it does not qualify a whole-tick improvement or any original performance target.

## Native validation joins shared preparation admission — 2026-10-23

`lunco-core-runtime::AsyncWorkAdmission::submit_transport` admits a native
transport to the existing bounded priority queue rather than requiring an
immediately free worker slot. It returns a scoped `QueuedWorkRequest`; dropping
that request cancels its exact queued incarnation, including when another
request reuses the same work key. The callback receives the real shared permit
and transfers it through its work and unconsumed result. CPU submissions share
the same enqueue/dispatch mechanism and retain their permit through execution.
Worker count, queue capacity, priority/fairness and required capacity are unchanged.
This generic scheduling/ownership boundary stays in Rust; no scenario retry,
new policy hook or secondary queue is introduced.

`lunco-scene-validation` uses that transport on native hosts. Its completion
channel has one slot. The request cancels queued work on retirement; dispatched
work checks the owner's lease before reading and a dropped receiver rejects
late completion. Permits remain live through an unconsumed result and the
retained Twin lint continuation. Source, mount, policy and runtime fences still
validate at serial publication. Browser validation uses its cooperative task
transport and immediate capacity reservation.

All **19 shared-admission tests** pass. New cases verify waiting behind occupied
capacity, holding capacity after the callback returns, exact-incarnation
cancellation and bounded waiting-queue rejection. Existing worker isolation,
fairness, required capacity and teardown tests also pass. The regular Tracy
production build passes in 4m17s. `modelica_source_target` now passes **20
authored checks** at tick 84, including source rejection, replacement, recovery
and participant cleanup. Its validation waits approximately **3,237 ms** in the
queue and starts with all three optional slots occupied, without resubmission.
`rhai_lint_policy` passes its **three checks** against the invalid authored
program and its owning rule. APIs 4980/4981 and PIDs 1949112/1950084 are closed.

Raw evidence remains ignored and local under
`.cache/codex-evidence/performance-20261023/`: `validation-queue-core-tests.log`,
`validation-queue-build-final.log`, and `validation-queue/{source-target,rhai-lint-policy}.{log,json}`.
This closes the validation gate; it does not establish a product loading,
physics or graphics gain. All original targets remain open. Continue with

## Isolated preparation execution — 2026-10-23

`lunco-core-runtime` executes admitted native preparation on one process-owned
Bevy `TaskPool`, separate from the native asset/shader async pool. Its width
follows the host's configured async worker count at first use. This adds a
bounded executor; admission limits, source validation, deterministic commits,
and scene retirement remain at their existing owners. The process lifetime
avoids joining a parser worker during scene teardown or joining the executor
from its own completing worker. No numerical settings or rendering quality
change. The saturation regression fails on the shared executor and passes with
isolation; all **17 async-work tests** and the regular Tracy production build
pass.

## Native physics migration — 2026-10-21

Bevy 0.20 and Avian 0.8 use the immutable, published dependency revisions in
[the integration review](upstream-only-physics-integration.md), including the
BigSpace mixed-child propagation fix. The regular production Tracy build passes
with normal `target/`, sccache and four jobs. Native Avian owns CCD, joints,
damping, motors and contact response. Modelica owns drivetrain/effect equations;
the typed Rust coupling checks units, frames, write authority and native range.

The current headless Roadster hull capture reports 2.720 ms mean PhysicsSchedule
time over its final 120 complete steps; CCD is 1.434 ms and native substeps
0.432 ms. Its retained API history has a 2.426 ms median. Restart leaves zero
contact pairs referencing missing/non-collider entities. Native export, file
framing and the actual 1 ms sampling period are verified. These are diagnostic
measurements; analytic-cylinder comparison and clean graphical acceptance are
still open.

Retained earlier captures qualify only their recorded artifact and inputs. They establish comparison baselines and do not qualify the migrated application.

repair admits one. All twelve focused tree tests pass, including source edits,
filter changes and scene teardown. The manual gate benchmark remains ignored.

new presentation policy or hook. Existing metadata and ancestry changes still
invalidate through their owner, stale revisions still reject publication, and

## Complete-cycle service fairness — 2026-10-20

The conditioned-contact runtime reaches physics step 345 and the timing
profile records 409 completed fixed cycles with `ready:true`, `faulted:false`.
The run is nevertheless failed acceptance: its screenshot never publishes
within 10 s. The owner profile exposes the cause: one app update drains 64
complete cycles in 6.331 s; a later one spends 6.238 s and retains 19.612 s
of pending demand. Readiness remains clear. This is a synchronous catch-up
batch, separate from the earlier terminal contact fault. The artifact is
Tracy-enabled, other sessions overlap, and these are not clean FPS results.

`lunco-time` now yields between complete cycles when the loop has spent one
fixed quantum of monotonic wall-clock service, while retaining its 64-cycle
hard ceiling. At least one admitted tick completes. Pending `overstep`, manual
step demand, causal receipt admission, fixed delta and input order remain at
their existing owners. Only the next host yield boundary depends on measured
service; no tick is shortened, skipped or reordered. The native and recording
hosts consume this same runner. This generic clock fairness invariant needs
no authored routing/selection hook or second clock. It does not solve the
cost of an individual slow physics tick.

The tiny-quantum low-level resource seam exercises a real dispatch yield,
pause, exact pending-time conservation, resumed completion and empty demand
without sleeps or a fake clock. The recording gate now observes completed
cycles yielding with pending demand rather than requiring a hardware-dependent
exact 64-cycle batch. The two clock resource-seam checks pass. The rendered production recording
observer passes six assertions and saves three PNGs, with no partial-debt
readback or clipped demand. Its first empty-stage attempt correctly remained
armed with no meshes and no fixed ticks; that attempt is invalid recording
acceptance. The real mesh fixture is `scenes/tests/point_instancer.usda`, whose
captured green cube is visually inspected.

Final production artifact
`b41ddfebd0f26b41581a2d4e3d45be8ee89e053c5586e93c490a254122b4746f`
passes 33 authored physics assertions plus expected invalid-joint rejection,
11 lifecycle assertions, and the six recording assertions. Its Tesla run
advances through 272 observed fixed cycles without a runtime fault, captures
idle and moving views, and admits/retires a stable-producer forward intent.
The input-surface throttle reads 1 while held and 0 after release; measured
speed reaches 7.187 m/s. Exact Twin hashes remain unchanged within each run and owned ports
are released. The final run is a different source cohort: concurrent work added
`models/JetFlightPilot.mo` and changed `models/RoadsterVehicle.mo` after the
conditioned-contact run. `tesla-source-cohorts.json` records both file hashes;

Twin changes are preserved. The 409-cycle no-fault observation uses the initial
cohort; the final driving and fairness observation uses the updated cohort. Recent Events shows the first fault in the retained failure
capture and simulation lifecycle notices in the validated transport gate.

This remains diagnostic, not goal acceptance: the artifact enables Tracy,
concurrent workloads are retained, and the final 120 Avian steps have a
73.210 ms median and 593.748 ms maximum during idle plus driving. The latest
loop completes one tick, takes 19.544 ms and retains 25.460 s of demand;
recorded API command latency peaks at 0.492 s. The queue is deliberately
visible and conserved; individual physics ticks are still too expensive.

## Rectangle-cover reuse and simulation lifecycle notices — 2026-10-20

`lunco-terrain-surface` shares one exact f64 slab clipper between bounded cache
preparation and the outer interval traversal. Preparation prefers one common
containing half; a segment crossing both midplanes instead receives a complete
clipped rectangle cover. Total cuts retain the existing eight-cut default,
exact-coordinate keys, FIFO residency and complete surface invalidation. The
cache owns reusable scratch storage; its two child slots are stack storage.
Cache hits do not choose the cut order or change query budgets/failure semantics.

The crossing-ray regression fails on the baseline: warming consumes 20 bounds
and three point evaluations, and three nearby rays increase those counts to
23 and 12. The complete-cover implementation warms at 19 bounds/zero point
evaluations and adds neither for those three rays. Its shifted blocked-ray
negative case and all eight certified-occlusion numerical tests pass, including
the shared-endpoint budget assertions. The final scratch-storage implementation
is covered by `rectangle-cover-final-test.log`. Product timing and Tracy
comparison remain pending for this phase.

`lunco-time::SimulationLifecycleChanged` owns discrete Started/Paused/Unpaused/
Stopped facts. The first automatic fixed tick establishes start; accepted
transport mode changes establish pause/unpause. Scene retirement retains only
one terminal stop notice until after scene-resource resets. Temporary holds,
single stepping and duplicate commands do not create transport edges.
`SetTimeTransport` validates the whole request before mutation, including a
combined invalid-rate/play request. `lunco-telemetry-core` projects these facts
to four stamped Info events; the application presentation bridge publishes
four Simulation notices through the existing Recent Events reader. This fixed
fact projection needs no authored selection hook. The private history/reset
bridge seam passes (`simulation-lifecycle-status-test.log`); the retained
11-assertion retained authored observer passes in both headless and windowed
production hosts (22 assertions), with each owned port released. The same normal
artifact passes the eight physical solar assertions and expected invalid-solar
fault boundary. Its archive is verified by decompressed SHA-256. No dependency or Tracy sources are changed.

## Physical celestial communication boundaries — 2026-10-19

`lunco-time::CelestialClock` evaluates the existing tick-rooted affine domain
at an explicit boundary, without using accumulated durations or a per-frame
resolved snapshot. `lunco-celestial-spatial` publishes interval-start inputs
before environment computation and endpoint poses after native writeback.
Initialization uses the current boundary. Its generic prescribed-pose fact is
owned by the analytical provider, stamped with the admitted cycle and active
frame, and consumed through the existing shared simulation-pose reader. Native
and prescribed ownership cannot coexist on one entity. Pending, stale, invalid
or differently framed samples remain unavailable. Removal retires ownership;
entity teardown retires the fact. Continuous frame math and phase/readiness
invariants belong to these engine owners, without a scenario policy hook.

Physical directions, gravity and solar visibility now use the same reader.
The visibility owner also converts probe origins and terrain rays through it.
Shared semantic axes cancel analytically, avoiding epoch-dependent roundoff in
local fields. The ephemeris provider retains its exact-epoch cache; rendered
body placement and illuminance retain their angular-error cadence. Physical
irradiance has one communication-boundary writer. No dependency source changes
or new integrator are involved.

The boundary seam passes at 100,000x with deliberately unrelated render poses,
including start/end epochs, exact local-frame cancellation, unchanged sample
publication and invalid clock withdrawal. Seven shared pose tests pass.
Regular `cargo run -j 4 -- --help` succeeds without compiler warnings. Production
initialization/pause/rate/multirate acceptance passes 43 checks at 120 Hz host
pacing. Solar visibility passes eight checks, and its negative admission gate
exits successfully on the declared terminal `solar-sample-unavailable` fault.

Fresh headless and GPU windowless Griffin missions pass 16 checks each with
clear readiness, zero runtime errors and clean API exits. The inspected image
retains rover, terrain and cast shadow. They use the frozen Twin `bbc0e77b`, the
same DEM as below, and binary SHA-256
`30e131970373f50534e610eac11d864e60bc610ff4445898ba5f6901c87dedb4`.
Through tick 5459, all 2,365 captured empty-input records match. Of 49,131
compared communication records, the first remaining difference is FLIP at step
3082, shortly after release at tick 3079; Lander differs from step 5428.
Completion is 5459 versus 5462. This removes the prior step-1441 divergence,
but does not qualify complete native-state replay. Full-sample investigation
of the release interval identifies differing native position, yaw/yaw-rate and
wheel-speed inputs, together with the resulting mount direction; the first
difference is not isolated to irradiance. Mechanical release/pose admission
requires investigation. Hash equality alone is not state proof.

Performance also remains open: the recent full-cycle medians are approximately
11.01 ms headless and 6.80 ms rendered, with authored-verdict spikes included
in the tails. These are diagnostic cohorts, with concurrent build activity;
they are neither native-physics service nor clean FPS acceptance. Evidence is
retained under `.cache/codex-evidence/celestial-sampling/`. The 200 FPS, sub-1 ms
native physics, complete presentation isolation and broader FMI qualification
requirements are not closed by this repair.

## Shared native mount and gravity owner — 2026-10-19

`SimulationPoseQuery` and immutable API reads share one bounded ownership
walk. Mounted entities compose their local chain below the nearest seeded
rigid body with its native f64 pose. An unseeded body or missing active frame
returns unavailable. The environment consumes this same boundary for both
consumer and provider poses; its independent native/world-position selection
is removed. Ancestor motion, hierarchy edits and readiness withdrawal
invalidate mounted fields. Provider poses are reused within the pass. Flat
fields retain missing-field admission, and surface invalidation visits only
linked consumers and existing fields. These are spatial/readiness invariants;
there is no scenario hook, special vehicle rule or dependency-source patch.

The mounted-pose seam fails before the repair and passes afterward. The
gravity resource seam also fails before cutover, then verifies native
translation/rotation, render independence, readiness/frame withdrawal and
restoration. Final review reproduces retained gravity after a mount loses
its parent; the removal-stream repair also withdraws transformless fields
without broadening flat-field invalidation. All six pose tests and three
gravity tests pass. Regular `cargo run -j 4 -- --help` succeeds without
compiler warnings; the skill
catalogue validates 43 entries. Evidence is retained under
`.cache/codex-evidence/mounted-probes/`.

Fresh headless and GPU windowless Griffin missions each pass 16 authored
checks with clear readiness, zero runtime errors and verified API exit/port
release. The captured image shows rover, terrain and cast shadow. Both use
Twin `bbc0e77b`, the pinned DEM below and binary SHA-256
`83698abec9d9b3258474b9870825dc0102b03596e66b108438beeac5ac1a4709`.
Through completion at tick 5459 in both hosts, 2,365 captured input records
match exactly and contain only empty physical intent frames. Across 49,131

1441; Lander also differs from step 5428. IMU/gravity fingerprints match

## Scenario source admission before simulation startup — 2026-10-12

source has no simulation hold before a document exists. Modelica reaches the
terminal fault at tick 18 before `on_start` checks the initial state or declares
the expected fault. A faster prepared participant can outrun source loading.

The scripting loader now reserves through `ScenarioPreparationAdmissions`
before awaiting assets. Document admission and compilation consume that same
key; initialization/startup release it. Pending sources hold actor activation
so load completion cannot select startup order. Typed path/handle pairs fence
replacement, and removed inputs/actors release their source reservations.
Document-only cancellation preserves loader-owned holds. Load, identity,
import or attachment failure produces `scenario-source-failed` at the owner.
This is an engine admission invariant, with no new policy hook or clock.

## Shader-source publication ownership — 2026-10-12

Shader-source reflection is independent of material binding. The render binder
publishes `ShaderLookSourceInterface` only through its reflection owner; retiring
or invalidating a material preserves the loaded-source facts. Source admission
precedes reflection, and material readiness invalidation precedes promotion.
No clocks, simulation math, rendering quality or external-library code change.

The baseline resource seam fails because readiness invalidation removes a valid
interface. The corrected seam passes material invalidation/retirement and actual
source removal. On normal baseline `faee8b646cdacd1e9a9935e97ebbfa20309a89cc6e213ac39d9bab7158b799fb`,
owned Summer Space School PID 2187473 starts with missing source reflection and
an invisible continuation. Reloading the unchanged terrain shader restores its
source facts and drawable continuation. The globe owner admits cutout only
with a drawable collar; missing reflection leaves the globe under the DEM.

Normal fixed artifact
`e657368fe23e06eb282229804a42c0c2ea403ff275fdc7fb0ca7370efaa11209`
builds in 2m 46s. Four startup observations retain valid source reflection and a
visible continuation; inspected final imagery has no flat patches. Earlier gate
attempts used WorldRoot without a simulation identity or a one-shot visualization
hook and are not passing test evidence. The production Rhai gate uses the existing
Twin-scoped scenario host with declared query reads. It passes all four checks,
including inactive-path rejection, active reload, preserved source identity and

## Native Modelica execution domains — 2026-10-12

Document constructors previously occupied scene participants' thread-affine
owners despite command-level step prioritization. The native worker now captures `ModelicaExecutionDomain` at compile dispatch: USD participants use
`Scene`; the existing document-created actor marker selects `Document`. Cached
reset/recompile work retains the captured domain. Preparation, scene and document
execution have independent startup-captured bounds; the default maximum is
6/6/1 threads. Admission uses the largest pool count plus two staged operations.
Sessions remain on their constructing thread, and completions retain submission
order. This is engine capacity isolation; no authored policy hook, new clock,
solver math, precision or external-library change is introduced.

The production Tracy build passes in 2m 23s. Exact artifact
`993909d2cb6da36833a24146d684b029f42a31374069603955b38bd00daf96b6`
runs the same fresh-cache four-model diagnostic on parent `95b87e23d`.

preparation-only diagnostic artifact
`8debe51ca3f1dc8e6a3cc6ad3a7b4905a1b18d95a815e16732c451653ec301da`
has a 140.093 ms maximum, including 140.081 ms of same-thread constructor work.

on the same owner. Independent preparation and document capacities address both
measured sources of interference.

diagnostic observations, not clean loading/FPS acceptance.

input transactions and admits checked observations in round order. Each native
session is constructed, stepped and retired on one thread. Session and library
generation fences reject stale construction receipts before publication.

## Exact-epoch ephemeris cache admission — 2026-10-11

The analytic provider's existing single-epoch cache admits 47 of 143 requests
in a settled eight-second High-quality sandbox interval. Its 96 misses are
first requests for their exact `(body_id, epoch_jd.to_bits())` samples: no
previously evaluated sample is requested again after eviction. The interval
covers 32 distinct epochs and Earth, EMB and Moon. A larger retained epoch set
would not eliminate any of these observed misses.

The preceding native baseline ranks `link_snapshot_occlusion_inputs` at
1.054 ms mean across 163 recomputes in the full capture. The pinned celestial
library's `Vsop2013Earth::heliocentric_position` privately evaluates EMB and
Moon operands; the application's exact-epoch cache cannot access those
intermediate samples through that call. Retain the maintained formula owner
and native f64 operation order rather than adding a second Earth calculation.
Continue with measured application-owned work, including the entity-tree gate.

## Avoid repeated Modelica wire-owner lookups — 2026-10-11

`lunco-modelica-telemetry` caches each channel's owner publication under a
session cursor for live ownership and the shared registry's catalog revision.
Unchanged admitted channels skip repeated string-keyed owner lookups. A changed
batch invalidates every channel's publication, including channels skipped for
non-finite values or capacity; only actual association acknowledges a channel.
Reuse also requires a retained scalar sample: preparing an owner or metadata
alone does not establish admitted wire ownership. New variables, owner changes
and removal/re-admission publish through `SignalRegistry`, which remains the
archived wire-identity owner. Sampling, histories, variable iteration, f64 values
and solver/physics timing retain their existing contracts. This is cache
admission in the existing projection owner, with no new policy hook or parallel
identity store; see [the telemetry contract](../architecture/telemetry-subsystem.md).

The focused Rust resource-seam test covers removal before the first scalar,
removal of an admitted channel, a new variable, changed source identity,
non-finite values across owner/catalog changes and preservation of archived
ownership when the live component disappears. The normal production artifact is
`195d19025df902d0c19442d61c0f6bce70eb0a19bd554f814bdb03c30b927a88`.
Its 18-check telemetry-history gate, stable API schema and scene restart pass.
All ten existing replay profiles pass 360 authored checks at compute widths
1/24, with 4/8/20 rovers, large seeds and jitter, against the unchanged exact
reference `9d4d03c5864a07d8db782959ab79256297e9d49761b073f4a3a23cd0f406c548`.
Owned normal PID 3786863 and all replay processes exit zero and release API
4202. The `modelica-owner-publication-complete-` receipts under
`target/entity-tree-perf/` retain exact artifacts/sources, native exports,
verdicts, workload observations and session closure.

A temporary native admission diagnostic observes 324 settled source batches
covering 73,692 eligible channel visits with zero owner-publication calls.
All nine live Modelica sources have a global owner. The 312,615,482-byte capture

## Native preprocessing binding lifetime — 2026-10-11

The native `prepare_preprocess_bind_groups` cost of 0.50–0.61 ms per settled
frame includes binding allocation, but allocation capacity is not a reuse key.
Bevy 0.19.1's `collect_buffers_for_phase` exchanges the per-phase resources
with the frame-level buffer tables, clearing and retaining the old allocations
for the next preparation. This alternates the actual indirect and phase-buffer
cohorts even when their sizes and scene counts stay constant. Native view groups
must also be available on each current extracted view before a preparation can
be skipped.

Two owned High-quality sandbox captures observe zero consecutive-frame
admissions: 2,969 and 2,874 misses. The complete binding-fact collection alone
costs 78.5 and 87.0 microseconds mean across these captures, so retaining that
gate would add work without removing native preparation. A sampled lifetime
capture verifies a same-size indirect CPU-metadata buffer changing from
`BufferId(2246)` to `BufferId(2256)` at 2,808 bytes, and missing native view groups
before preparation. RenderDevice changes once at startup and remains unchanged
on the following 3,006 checks. Pipeline-cache change ticks cannot establish
binding invalidation: its native queue-processing system mutably accesses it
on every render frame; use actual layout and resource facts.

The `preprocess-bindings-` receipts under `target/entity-tree-perf/` retain the
three native captures (392,838,102; 393,727,331; 400,289,810 bytes), successful
native exports, exact artifact/source hashes and owned-process checks. The
lifetime summary separates the two admission captures from the sampled
invalidation diagnostic. Each owned app/collector exits zero and releases API
4202 and Tracy 8087. Instrumentation and the ineffective gate are removed;
production Rust sources match their admitted revision byte for byte. Package
cleanup removes only this checkout's root-package outputs, and the verified
normal artifact
`3e40a55f3f8c6121508770a8fb977438789f37ad137cf66f0b658e95ef491952`
is restored. Its unchanged 360-check replay and 18-check history/restart evidence
remains valid. No product speedup, loading improvement or new determinism
acceptance is claimed by these diagnostic captures.

The current binding-construction owner remains native. Any future reuse must
cover the actual buffer cohort, binding offsets/ranges, layout descriptors,
occlusion textures, work modes/counts and bin-job parameters, and publish
complete groups to current views at the native deferred boundary. A one-frame
latch or a capacity-only key does not meet that contract. Continue with measured
application-owned costs rather than adding that extra per-frame work.

## Measure local-shadow reuse candidacy — 2026-10-11

Native exports show twelve nonempty 4096-by-4096 local maps, with three to six
multidraw sets per map in the settled integrated capture. The ground and rover
materials consume native PBR lighting, so these maps have real readers.
The application delegates depth rendering to Bevy; its native shadow atlas
allocation cache retains textures, while preparation assigns sorted light
layers and creates each frame's clearing depth attachments.

An owned diagnostic capture measures sorted native visible caster identities,
mesh identities, GPU pose/geometry-index bits, skin indices and the light's
extracted projection/transform. In three settled eight-second View/Builder/View
intervals, ten maps change this digest every sampled frame. Maps 10 and 11
repeat it on 232/507, 200/457 and 183/572 frames (46%, 44% and 32%). The
candidate lists contain 4–73 shadow casters and no skinned instances. These are
visibility candidates, not an inventory of ready draw items. Stable draw-set
counts do not establish a static depth image. A cache keyed by these candidates
would cover only part of two maps, leaving most measured GPU cost in place.

These diagnostic digests do not admit a render cache: material/pipeline state,
mesh contents, morphing, backing texture identity and atlas-layer assignment
still require independent invalidation. Native preparation shares the spot
atlas with directional cascades, so a light-count or cascade change can shift
layers without changing the light's entity identity. No passes are skipped and
no scene, shader, simulation, quality or external-library source changes remain.

The 407,955,766-byte capture and successful native exports use the
`shadow-pose-diagnostic-` prefix under `target/entity-tree-perf/`. Owned app and
collector PIDs 3374477/3374476 exit zero and release API 4202/Tracy 8087. The
workload snapshots contain only those processes. Temporary instrumentation is
removed with exact source-byte checks, and the normal artifact
`3e40a55f3f8c6121508770a8fb977438789f37ad137cf66f0b658e95ef491952`
is restored and verified. Its existing 360-check replay and 18-check history/
restart evidence remains valid. The preceding native CPU export ranks
`prepare_preprocess_bind_groups` at 0.50–0.61 ms mean per settled frame; inspect
application view admission and its native binding-resource lifetime next.
This capture does not establish a product performance gain or complete acceptance.

## Reuse pending-source query metadata — 2026-10-11

`lunco-usd-sim-cosim` retains Bevy's system-local `QueryState` for Modelica
source admission. Normal iteration refreshes new archetypes and returns current
pending entities; sampling, source ordering, wiring and initialization stay at
their existing boundaries. This is an engine metadata mechanism with an existing
repository consumer pattern, so it adds no policy hook or parallel admission path.

Native Tracy exports compare the same High-quality sandbox and camera in three
settled eight-second View/Builder/View intervals. The admission system's body
median falls from 92.162/91.135/81.532 to 1.823/1.744/1.613 microseconds.
Both owned app/collector pairs exit zero and release API 4202 and Tracy 8087.

## Move asynchronous Modelica syntax copies to parse workers — 2026-10-11

`lunco-modelica-core` queues each worker's typed syntax cache at the session
installation boundary. The update-side consumer moves its shared AST,
generation and located diagnostics directly, validates the document generation,
and installs it once. Native workers prepare the session's separate owned AST
off-lock. The existing FIFO, wake latch, pending admission, index owner and
class-change consumer remain in place. Completion budgeting now includes the
index installation and stops the drain after a result exhausts the budget.
WASM transport and prepared-library results share that document consumer;
the browser page still performs its session ownership copy. This is a generic
resource/ownership mechanism, with no new lifecycle policy or Rhai hook.

and index-install tails cannot be paired here: the checkout's entire `target/`

`lunco-luncosim-edit-ui::ui::entity_list::scene_topology_changed` now filters

for those identities. Quiet archetypes do not prepare optional label, hierarchy
and visibility data. Native source admissions cover additions while the five
mutable metadata filters protect current and pending snapshots. Removals,
parent edges, scene boundaries and Twin retirement keep their owning checks. The existing dirty worker consumes the same boolean.
This projection change introduces no new policy,
revision owner, simulation action, clock admission or ordering.

`entity_tree_gate_parent_changes`. Both artifacts in the final comparison have

explicit `SetCameraLookAt` command, exactly equal camera poses and

`TelemetryRetentionPlugin`. Native and browser workers return current values
through the same ordered result path. `ModelicaSessionStarted` carries the exact
installed actor/session identity to presentation; UI binding cleanup validates

## Admit only relevant gravity work — 2026-10-11

`lunco-environment` separates flat-field admission from surface-field
invalidation. Steady surface work selects linked consumers and existing
fields; unlinked spatial entities without `LocalGravity` cannot produce a
field or remove one. A flat field admits only missing spatial fields between
owner invalidations. Gravity, active-frame, provider and link-removal changes
still check all spatial entities. Native-position changes, body-relative
surface math, f64 precision, deferred publication and simulation cadence
remain unchanged. This is an engine derivation mechanism over the existing
typed gravity facts, with no changeable policy or new hook. Dependencies are
unchanged.

`cargo test -p lunco-environment --lib gravity -j 4` passes all three checks
(12 filtered). The existing flat-field check also covers late admission,
field recreation, resource edits and unresolved surface cleanup. The native
surface check retains astronomical-ancestor/render-pose isolation and native
position invalidation, and now covers link removal and re-admission.

cache. Declared owners without slots still block lower-precedence owners;

capacity and unavailable host capacity fail through structured worker errors.

`target/image-loader-perf/` and `target/perf/` artifacts referenced below are
therefore unavailable locally. Their descriptions record historical results;
new entity-tree timing receipts and accepted traces are retained under
`target/entity-tree-perf/`. Later task-owned space recovery removes superseded

export is compressed; its source trace and settled summary remain available.

## Participating-closure prepared-solve reuse — 2026-10-10

`lunco-modelica-worker` keys prepared solve IR by the strict compiler's
participating-source content identity, resolved solver and exact override bits.
Source-root admission still invalidates compiled artifacts and preserves all
ordered completion fences. Bounded prepared graphs remain reusable after a
fresh strict compilation yields the same closure. Native storage owns only the
current `prepared-solve-v8` namespace. Compiler admission revision remains
diagnostic context, outside prepared-model identity. This is generic immutable
cache ownership, with no policy hook or external-library change.

The inline-source regression fails before the change and passes afterwards:
admitting an unrelated library changes compiler revision but preserves exact
initial values, parameters and solve identity; editing the participating
library changes both identity and parameter values. All 64 worker tests pass,
including malformed-record rejection, identity validation, bounded retention
and ordered scheduling. The production native build and browser worker compile
check pass. The browser check reports the existing unused
`BackendCompileResult.unit` field; browser runtime acceptance is not claimed.

Two owned High-quality sandbox sessions use isolated cold caches and the same
authored scene, then replace it once. Baseline PID 2266553/API 4200 performs two
redundant replacement lowerings; changed PID 2280283/API 4200 performs none and
all nine replacement preparation observations are memory hits. Distinct disk
records fall from ten to eight. Byte-sliced log comparison verifies matching
model/source identities and the same initial solver and override vectors.
Initial readiness is 11.165/11.471 s and replacement readiness 1.034/0.962 s;
these single observations do not establish a causal loading-time or FPS gain.
Cold rover lowering and preparation queue waits remain open.

The exact changed production artifact
`c64c3acf84ff5b811a6c327260fd383ae2205eb269b29086b00a0339a7960efa`
passes ten production Rhai replay profiles with 360 authored checks, including
deliberate mismatch rejection, against the unchanged reference. Four-, eight-

Both native artifacts have hash-verified gzip archives. Evidence lives under

`prepared-solve-key-current-replay-20261010.json`.

An early readiness probe and a disk-full replay/check attempt have no acceptance
verdict. The successful retry supersedes them. Recovery removes only unused

## Relative shadow-pose evidence — 2026-10-09

Owned normal PID 1559815/API 4185 uses the prior exact normal artifact
`9c5d65cc450467b491fa12a8c0984afa57c4fc8078276de77905b3274d2f98c3`.
After full readiness and twelve seconds of settling, 24 production API snapshots
over 4.25 seconds contain all 206 meshes and twelve spotlights. The camera and
physics topology stay fixed while physics advances. All render affines change;
198 mesh positions, 133 mesh rotations, twelve light positions and eight light
rotations also change in the active simulation frame. Four lights have 25
exactly stable active-frame relative mesh poses each, but no complete scene or
light has stable relative render affines. These comparisons invert the sampled
affines in double precision; they are not GPU shader bit-equivalence tests.

Native spot shadow views additionally derive their basis from forward direction,
not full authored roll. Native preparation creates current depth attachments
and clears them at first use each frame. The measured inputs therefore do not
justify unchanged-map reuse, and no shadow cache is added. The snapshot and
analysis files are `target/image-loader-perf/shadow-relative-pose-samples-20261009.json`
and `shadow-relative-pose-analysis-20261009.json`. App exit is zero and port
4185 closes. A later screenshot request occurs after its bounded session
already closes and supplies no image; source and shadow draws are unchanged.

## Retained fixed-cycle demand — 2026-10-09

`lunco-time` admits the complete running virtual delta and drains at most 64
complete causal cycles per app update. All remaining duration stays in
`Time<Fixed>::overstep`; pause, solver and scene holds retain that balance.
The fixed timestep, serial World access, per-cycle admission check, ordered
physics/Modelica/Rhai work and completed-tick calendar are unchanged. The
telemetry query reports the full balance as
`fixed_loop.latest_pending_simulation_secs`. Manual scene-test and recording
consumers share this owner; the recorder waits for every whole admitted cycle
before readback. The standalone Lunica composition no longer overwrites its
workbench-installed clock. Current contracts are in
[unified time](../architecture/19-unified-time-and-clock.md) and
[offline recording](../offline-recording.md).

is admitted instead of 10 ms. It now passes at 0.1x/1x/64x, proving exact
completed-plus-pending conservation, the 64-cycle work bound, pause retention
and full later draining with no additional input duration. All 54 existing
and replacement time-owner tests pass using that same built test binary.
No repository/Twin asset is embedded in these generic Rust tests.

The normal production artifact is SHA-256
`9c5d65cc450467b491fa12a8c0984afa57c4fc8078276de77905b3274d2f98c3`.

## Physics metadata ownership — 2026-10-09

`lunco-usd-sim-telemetry` retains a publication flag per cached signal and moves
new metadata into `SignalRegistry`. Descriptions, units, provenance, groups and
presentation have one value owner. Per-source identity, global-owner tracking,
path invalidation, scalar values and sampling clocks retain their contracts.
In the observed 1,954-channel physics catalog, this removes 1,954 full metadata
copies and at least 7,816 owned string copies (four non-empty fields per channel,
with compound-presentation strings additional). This is a source-proven work and
storage reduction, not a measured product speedup.

The finer baseline trace is
`target/image-loader-perf/telemetry-retention-phases-20261009.tracy` (SHA-linked
executable `3b7894c5fb4bdb0ba2af8beafe37f02ed6d8783d54894c980a69be7dc2887b66`,
PID 1323696/API 4176, collector 1323695). It finds 4,007 channel creations,
maximum 0.921 ms, including a 0.420 ms creation at map size 1,792. Physics
metadata spans total 2.981 ms; source retention peaks at 1.368 ms. The prior
17.637 ms source spike does not recur, so its origin remains unproven.
The after trace uses executable
`faa3a4958a78682bcff3181fd5e3289c73ff78090c3578392d5e8d21f3c57deb`,
PID 1326661/API 4177 and collector 1326660. The same 1,954 metadata spans

## Owned shader-raster loading — 2026-10-09

`lunco-materials` prepares decoded, owned images on the async-compute pool
before publishing typed `PreparedShaderImage` roots and their native image
children. `ShaderTexture` retains each colour/scalar/normal source independently
and preserves native reload dependencies. Extension-free registration leaves
native image and glTF decoding/settings intact. The render binder consumes
complete children and reacts to source events; its resident-pixel snapshot,
task/version registry and mip installation pass are removed. Prepared children
use native render-only extraction, moving bytes into GPU preparation while
retaining main-world descriptors. Generated terrain/annotation images retain
their native ownership. The canonical contract is in
[shader layers](../architecture/shader-layers-and-params.md).

The focused production-feature test command
`cargo test -p lunco-materials -p lunco-luncosim --lib image_ -j 4` passes ten
tests, including initial/reload complete chains, independent same-file roles,
native loader settings, sampler quality, render-only child usage, invalid PNG
and invalid pixels, plus shared pixel math. Inline codec fixtures exercise the
asset seam without repository/Twin paths. Evidence is in
`target/perf/image-raster-source-seams-20261009.log`.
`ShaderTexture::load_raster` accepts physical raster paths and rejects container
labels with a typed error before issuing a prepared-root request. USD shader
admission consumes that error through the existing texture-source diagnostic.
Importer-owned labeled images use `ShaderTexture::Image`; an inline native
container-loader test verifies their load, binding and reload, and verifies that
a rejected raster request starts no prepared-root load. Physical asset identity
and importer-owned labels remain separate contracts.

`d8fa3aa3459fe09438760c7ddf96821bf08f6d541c034ea07a780ea7f9c6535f`.

an ownership investigation requiring per-source attribution and retained
reader semantics before an optimization, not evidence that the image usage
choice can be applied indiscriminately to meshes.

## sRGB mip filtering — 2026-10-09

`lunco-materials::rgba8_mip_chain` decodes the 256 possible sRGB byte values
once per colour chain instead of repeating the nonlinear transfer for every
sample. It reuses the same native f32 transfer function, sample order,
summation, encoding, alpha averaging and mip extents. Linear/normal chains do
not build the table. This is shared pixel math consumed by shader-raster
asset loading and terrain preparation; it adds no policy, hook,
dependency, API, cache lifecycle or simulation scheduling boundary.
The [canonical shader contract](../architecture/shader-layers-and-params.md)
and profiling skill describe the owner and evidence boundaries.

Seven native tests pass, including invalid inputs, normal/linear filtering,
non-power-of-two/rectangular extents and an sRGB comparison covering 65,536 byte pairs
against the transfer formula. The pure standard-library module was compiled
directly with the repository toolchain using
`rustc --edition 2024 --test -C opt-level=2 crates/lunco-materials/src/image_mips.rs -o target/perf/image-mips-mechanism-20261009`,
then `target/perf/image-mips-mechanism-20261009`;
the production builds separately validate package integration. A direct
before/after comparison also verifies every byte of a 44,739,244-byte,
13-level synthetic 4096×2048 chain. Its source and verdict are retained in
`target/perf/image-mips-full-byte-comparison-20261009.{rs,log}`.

The isolated same-toolchain/same-input A/B/B/A workload runs three repetitions
per process. Before median filtering is 291.95/290.28 ms; after is
88.08/88.46 ms, about 70% lower. Input construction and output verification
remain outside the timed filter. Full outputs have matching level counts,
sizes and checksums; the separate direct byte comparison above is stronger
than checksum identity. Source, executable identity and timings are retained
in `image-mips-bench-*-20261009.*`. These are owner-workload results, not
loading or FPS acceptance.

Temporary per-phase spans identify the production startup spike as base
snapshot copying: two 4096×2048 RGBA8 images copy 64 MiB on the app thread,
with one copy taking 51.14 ms. Event handling, request discovery, polling and
installation are small; mip filtering already runs off-thread. Owned before
PID 1079602/API 4161 has two worker jobs at 427.80/382.16 ms; after PID
1087314/API 4162 has 154.63/162.27 ms. Both install two results and pass
readiness, diagnostics, camera-audit stability, physics progress/topology and
visual shadow/geometry checks. Their verified owned 8086 listeners and
collector connections identify the captures. Both apps/collectors exit zero
and the API ports close. The after snapshot copies are 6.75/5.15 ms; no
snapshot mechanism changed in that measurement pair, so this variability
does not prove a copy reduction. The owned-loading step above removes that
resident snapshot at its asset owner.

## Telemetry recording and admission — 2026-10-09

`lunco-modelica-telemetry` checks live history existence only when the scalar
catalog is at its configured limit. Below that limit it records directly and
reads `SignalRegistry::scalar_count` after acceptance, removing one repeated
path hash per variable without another cache or API. A full catalog still
admits existing channels, including after the configured limit is lowered;
removed history must pass admission again. The generic registry/producer seam
`channel_limit_uses_live_registry_admission_after_removal` passes (one test,
six filtered), including removal and zero-limit rejection. No public command
removes that registry history, so that lifecycle boundary is tested at the
resource seam. The production history gate remains authored in Rhai.

remains at the existing stage/path/generation/full-profile boundary. The cache
holds weak handles, releases retired readers/assets through events, and leaves
physics geometry and authoritative scheduling unchanged. Quality changes
replace shared assets; point instances follow their last inherited handle
after retessellation while retaining appearance and private edits. Horizon UV
installation and NURBS edits detach from shared assets before mutation. The
[canonical contract](../architecture/render-decoupling.md#immutable-primitive-mesh-assets)
records the owner and why existing terrain/globe/material caches do not cover it.

retirement and quality-key replacement; horizon UV isolation with native
shadow reception; and point-instance inheritance/private-edit retention.

## Shadow GPU attribution — 2026-10-09

`lunco-render-bevy` composes native shadow systems between begin/end timestamps
through Bevy's existing render diagnostics recorder. Installation occurs only
for diagnostics-enabled render hosts, after native plugin registration. The
native delegate retains its implicit dependency set; PBR's system-local
before/after rules are reapplied explicitly. Begin validates every native
parameter before opening a span, and the read-only pipe keeps begin/native/end
on one CPU thread and queues their render contexts in that order. Ordinary
builds retain the native systems. No external source, simulation schedule,
clock, equation, precision, shadow draw, map resolution or quality setting
changes.

The focused schedule seam passes. It covers dependencies targeting the native
set and those owned by the native system, a single delegate call, paired-thread
execution, deferred buffer order, and nonmatching-view admission without an
orphan span. The production Tracy build passes. The owned corrected capture
verifies PID 886934, API 4142 and the matching 8086 profiler connection. View,
Builder and View pass readiness and physics-progress/topology checks. Ground
and object shadows, geometry and framing match the baseline screenshot on

All four groups are present, with twelve shared views per camera event apart

acceptance, and they establish no new speed gain.

## Selective live port declaration discovery — 2026-10-09

`lunco-port-core` owns `PortDeclarationQuery`, consumed by every backend's
`declare_ports` callback. Inspection collects the complete declaration rows in
their existing order. A named query borrows the exact name and causality side,
retaining only its first matching direction and slot; map-backed owners use their name
index directly. The fixed propagation consumer no longer builds and copies the
owner's complete surface to validate one resolved target. Live owner precedence,
metadata, writability, bounds, revision and slot validation still run before the
exclusive commit. This is a generic discovery mechanism, with no new policy
hook, scheduler, cache or numerical-state change.

The owned unprofiled sandbox pair used the same scene and topology. Realtime
fixed-tick service medians fell from 2.222/2.272 ms to 1.694/1.695 ms. Max-speed
capacity rose from 271.04 to 297.19 steps/s; its fixed-tick median fell from
1.519 to 1.084 ms. Realtime cadence remained near 60 steps/s. The exact before
and after binary hashes, commands and raw samples are in
`target/perf/ports-result-20261009.json` and `ports-{before,after}-20261009.json`.

A separate owned Tracy capture measured propagation means of
0.143/0.140/0.137 ms in settled View/Builder/View windows. The earlier diagnostic
capture measured 0.626/0.645/0.620 ms. These profiler captures have different
cadence and are attribution evidence; the unprofiled pair establishes the
fixed-tick service saving. Modelica output publication now costs approximately
0.25–0.28 ms per tick in this capture, exceeding propagation.

The port-core resource/registry seam suite passes 20 tests, including exact-name
and direction selection, owner precedence, invalid values, atomic rejection,
and stale/removed locators. Production Rhai passes the Earth-balloon propagation
gate 3/3 at 720 ticks and the negative duplicate-owner gate 11/11. The production
and Tracy builds pass without new warnings. Format and diff review are complete.

High-quality 1280×720 rendering still measures roughly 30–31 ms frame medians;
full Avian step medians remain approximately 0.91–0.93 ms. The GPU remains near
98–99% utilization with its software power cap active at about 20 W. Screenshots
retain the same visible scene and shadows, and camera inventories were recorded;
no exact camera-pose audit was performed. Warm clear-readiness observations were
3.046 s before and 2.138 s after on the process clock, but this pair does not

The existing exact multi-rover replay reference mismatch remains open. No

## Causal completion admission — 2026-10-09

The Modelica execution host admits worker completions in `First`, after message
rotation and before `ClockProjectionSet` samples the causal hold. Compile intent
stays in the `Update` lifecycle cycle. The worker keeps its existing session,
source, step, communication-endpoint and all-participant checks; fixed-clock
overstep, timestep, solver settings and physics equations are unchanged. This
placement is an engine admission invariant rather than a changeable Rhai policy.

An owned unprofiled A/B from `28c56ac25` measured the same sandbox topology
(50 bodies, 33 colliders, 20 joints). Headless realtime cadence increased from
51.4–51.5 to 60.1–60.2 steps/s in the two measured windows. Median fixed-tick
work remained approximately 2.2 ms. Max-speed capacity was 270 versus 263
steps/s; this diagnostic does not establish a raw solver or physics-cost gain.
Rendered High-quality, 1280×720 View/Builder windows increased from roughly
46 to 60 physics steps/s. Median frame time remained around 30–31 ms, and
median full Avian step cost remained around 0.92 ms. The GPU was software
power capped at about 20 W. Visual FPS and the 0.5 ms physics target remain open.

The eight focused worker bridge tests and the execution-host ordering test
pass, including stale-session rejection before clock projection. The production
Earth-balloon Rhai gate passes 3/3 checks at 720 ticks. Its declaration and the
Python-chain test declaration now name the entities they read. The chain reaches
a failure verdict in this default build, which does not enable its optional
Python backend; it is not admission validation evidence. Exact multi-rover replay
already fails on unchanged main because the reference expects 434 Modelica
variables while the current model exposes 438. The reference is unchanged;
full replay acceptance remains required.

Raw evidence is under `target/perf/solver-handoff-{main,after}-20261009.json`,
`target/perf/sandbox-{main-28c56ac25,admission-after}-20261009.json`, and the
`admission-*-20261009.log` files. All owned ports 4122–4129 are closed.
These runs establish a cadence improvement, not completion of the loading,
visual FPS, raw physics-cost or determinism objectives. The selective declaration
discovery section above records the subsequent port-propagation improvement.

> Status: Open · Worktree: optimization · Scene: Summer Space School Apollo

## Objective

Current acceptance is at least 150 FPS in Summer Space School and sandbox at
High visual quality, with the full Avian physics step below 0.5 ms per fixed
step. Use `PhysicsTotalDiagnostics.step_time`, surfaced as
`engine-health.physics_step_ms` and the recent one-per-step history returned by
`PhysicsPerformance.step_time_samples_ms`, as the acceptance metric; record its
p50/p95/p99/max and use Tracy subzones for attribution. Measure three startup
milestones separately: the app becomes ready in less than 2 s of process launch;
each Twin scene mounts in less than 2 s of the accepted Twin-open request; each
Twin reaches full readiness within 2 s of that request. App and Twin readiness
use separate clocks and separate evidence. App readiness has a 2 s process
clock; full Twin readiness has a 2 s Twin-open clock. Scene mount is a separate
2 s Twin-open checkpoint.

For an auto-opened Twin, use the `StartupScene opened` event as the Twin-open
origin. Preserve authored visuals, deterministic physics cadence, BigSpace
frame semantics, and responsive UI; do not meet these targets by reducing
quality, dropping authoritative ticks, or changing simulation semantics.
Use process launch as the app clock and Twin-open as the Twin clock.

For measurement, app-ready requires both the window-created event and a
successful `/api/health` response within 2 s of process launch; `/api/health`
by itself proves liveness.
Twin scene-mounted requires the live canonical stage and mounted scene root.
Full Twin-ready requires the scene load to finish, the scene-participant
lifecycle-ready event, and completion of every startup readiness producer's
initial registration pass. At that point, all active readiness items must drain
with `readiness_tracked=true`, `ready=true`, `world_hold=false`, `faulted=false`,
and `pending_count=0`. Poll readiness during startup; count the first sample
after the admission pass that satisfies all these conditions as the transition
only if the state remains clear for the next five seconds. The first green
`/api/ready` sample alone is not sufficient: current startup can register
scene, physics, and Modelica waits after an earlier green response. The
five-second soak validates the transition; report the elapsed target from the
first qualifying sample, not from the end of the soak. If a later wait appears,
reject the earlier sample and measure again after that wait clears.
Historical entries that record `/api/ready` samples describe that API response
only unless they track every startup producer through completion and verify no
later regression.

### 2026-10-05 — bounded editor and live-exchange work

Four application-owned changes preserve the existing mechanisms and policy:

- `lunco-sysml-ui` compares the active Twin identity, root, manifest, and file
  index before treating a workspace notification as projection invalidation.
  SysML source, analysis, and evidence changes still invalidate the view.
- `lunco-doc-bevy` checks every exact file origin before resolving aliases;
  SysML source links use that canonical registry lookup. There is no parallel
  path index, and document origins remain authoritative after rename/close.
- `lunco-scripting` borrows unchanged script documents during preparation and
  copies worker inputs only for admitted compile/retry work. Eligibility,
  submission order, result admission, and lifecycle commits are unchanged.
- `lunco-port-core` prepares a resolved input write once and commits within
  that exclusive World boundary. It avoids a second live validation and a
  single-element batch allocation. Validation ordering, rejected-write results,
  f64 values, precedence, stale handles, and batch atomicity are retained.

The named SysML workspace-focus, document-origin lookup, resolved-input safety,
and reverse-worker-completion lifecycle seam tests pass. The last test forces
`beta` to finish before `alpha` and verifies ordered `alpha, beta` commits.
These are focused mechanism checks, not whole-runtime determinism acceptance.

### 2026-09-30 — share fixed-weld lookup across mobility force systems

The fixed-step mobility pass previously scanned all fixed joints and allocated a
dynamic-body set separately for suspension and drive. It now refreshes one
retained-capacity set before the ordered force chain and shares it across both
consumers. Rollback replay refreshes the same owner resource before replayed
forces, preserving the live-joint decision at each deterministic tick boundary.
This removes one duplicate joint scan and set construction from each force
pass. `cargo +nightly-2026-02-27 check --locked --offline -j 4 -p
lunco-mobility` passed. No tests or post-change profile were run, so no
frame-time or physics gain is claimed.

### 2026-09-30 — reuse stable workbench anchor keys

The title-bar and dock layout previously created a fresh anchor vector and
allocated key strings for stable menu, toolbar, window, and panel anchors on
every egui pass. The workbench now retains that scratch vector in its system
local, borrows compile-time keys, and publishes registered panel anchors by
`PanelId`. Static anchor maps retain capacity across the existing per-frame
clear, while dynamic menu and perspective labels keep their owned-key path.
Guided anchors still reflect the active layout each pass, so closed panels do
not leave stale targets. This removes repeated small allocations from the UI
cycle; it is a focused steady-state reduction and is not claimed to explain the
full reported Builder/View FPS gap.
